# Otogrow OS improvement tracker

Last updated: 2026-11-03. This is the authoritative tracker for the redesign and critique follow-up. It supplements the original phase roadmap; existing local foundations do not mean these improvements are complete.

## Authorization and completion rules

The user asked to list and track every UI/UX and architecture criticism, complete them, and make the current system easier to use. This authorizes planning, implementation and verification. Continue in manageable batches without repeating phase approval requests. Preserve the existing spending limit and obtain missing provider/setup details when a real integration needs them.

The user reaffirmed autonomous completion on 2026-10-25: “no need to get confirmation from me now on finish all of the things needed”. Do not stop at a completed batch merely to offer the next batch or ask for approval. Continue implementation and verification in dependency order; report external prerequisites precisely and continue independent work. Routine status questions do not cancel the implementation objective.

On 2026-10-27 the user explicitly authorized continuing all 33 unfinished improvements without approval, with tracker updates and requests only for necessary input. Current counts remain evidence-based; a new work slice is not whole-item completion.

There are **46 items**: the original 42 findings, plus person-level activity history, AI analysis, phone restrictions and the subsequently requested internal team chat. The new dynamic-role and effective-access-preview requirements expand IMP-39 rather than duplicate it. The UI/UX summary maps to IMP-01 through IMP-12 and IMP-42.

Statuses: **Queued** = recorded but not started; **In progress** = active implementation; **Partial** = some verified improvement, acceptance still incomplete; **Local complete** = every listed acceptance check passed locally; **Blocked** = a specific external prerequisite prevents the next step. Local complete does not mean deployed or production accepted. Count an item once, and never count a partial fix as complete.

For every implementation update, record the date, changed behavior, tests/evidence, limitations and next step in the execution log. Keep stable IDs even if work is split into smaller tasks. Update this tracker before reporting progress to the user. Never store credentials here.

## Mandatory business, workflow and design review for every item

User instruction, 2026-10-25: think deeply about each item's effect on the company and its workflows, question whether it is the right change, and implement the best version for Otogrow. The user explicitly extended this to UI, UX, design structure and design ideas: design quality is a first-class requirement for every item, not a cosmetic pass after functionality. This rule applies to every item and subtask, including delegated work. Completing a checklist entry is not sufficient evidence that the business problem has been solved or that the experience is clear and usable.

Before implementation, perform a proportionate but substantive review and record the decision and its supporting evidence:

1. **Define the business outcome.** Identify who needs the change, the actual problem, and the expected effect on delivery speed, creative quality, client experience, founder/Amna workload, capacity or margins. Choose an observable success check; distinguish expected benefits from measured results.
2. **Trace the complete workflow.** Map the current and proposed flow, including upstream inputs, responsible people/agents, handoffs, approvals, client interaction, exceptions and downstream consequences. Check whether the change removes a bottleneck or merely transfers work to someone else. Consider the affected service types and roles, not only the founder's screen or the happy path.
3. **Challenge the proposed solution.** Inspect the current implementation and requirements, test assumptions, and compare credible alternatives such as reusing existing capabilities, changing the process or building a new feature. The listed solution is a hypothesis to evaluate. If it is unnecessary, harmful or superseded, record the evidence and propose a better resolution; never silently drop the requirement or mark an unimplemented item complete.
4. **Assess the whole system.** Examine effects on related modules, data ownership, permissions and multiple-role access, human accountability, integrations, costs, reporting and existing records. Preserve explicit business rules, including independent final approval, scoped client communications and spending controls. Plan migration, failure recovery and rollback where relevant.
5. **Design the best complete experience.** Evaluate information architecture, navigation, page structure, terminology, visual hierarchy, typography, spacing, density, component patterns and interaction design together. Compare credible design ideas and select the clearest approach for Otogrow's non-technical staff and clients, explaining why it fits their tasks. Make the current state, next action, owner and result understandable; reduce unnecessary decisions, clicks and repeated entry. Design loading, empty, error, success, permission and recovery states as part of the flow. Check responsive layouts, keyboard/focus behavior, contrast and reduced motion. Maintain a coherent design system across the OS; reuse good patterns and improve poor ones deliberately. Backend-only changes must still assess their effects on latency, feedback, permissions and the user journey. More cards, animation or visual polish alone do not establish better UX.
6. **Choose the best practical implementation.** Optimize for a clear, complete user journey, reliable behavior, maintainability and realistic growth within Otogrow's constraints. Explain meaningful tradeoffs. Reuse sound infrastructure, avoid unnecessary complexity, and connect the UI, backend, permissions and operational behavior. Cosmetic fixes, placeholders and partially connected features do not satisfy a larger workflow requirement.
7. **Verify the outcome and consequences.** Define business, workflow and UX acceptance checks before coding, then validate the affected roles and workflows with appropriate tests and hands-on inspection. For UI changes, inspect the actual screens at relevant desktop and narrow sizes and walk through the interactions; passing code tests alone is insufficient. Include relevant errors, revisions, revoked access and recovery paths. Check adjacent workflows for regressions and whether the intended business and usability improvements are supported by evidence. Record anything that still needs real staff or production acceptance, and refine the design when inspection reveals friction or inconsistency.

Before marking an item complete, add a concise decision record under its ID: **problem and business outcome; current → proposed workflow; alternatives and chosen approach; UI/UX and design rationale; cross-system effects and tradeoffs; functional and design acceptance evidence; remaining limits.** Record conclusions and evidence, not private deliberation. Related items may share a review, but each must identify its own impact and acceptance evidence. A material implementation or design change requires revisiting the decision. Completion requires both working behavior and an inspected, coherent experience for the people using it.

This is a quality requirement, not an additional user-approval gate. Continue authorized work autonomously; ask only when an unresolved business decision or external action genuinely requires the user's input. Do not invent business policy, authorize extra spending or widen scope in the name of producing the “best” version.

Earlier completion records do not establish that this new review has happened. Review previously completed items against this rule before relying on them in dependent work or final rollout; record the review and reopen any material gaps. Implementation counts are unchanged by this documentation update.

## Execution order

1. **Usability and notification correctness:** IMP-01–06, 09–12, 42. First slice: one navigation model, readable page names, reachable setup, task links, accurate readiness and hidden-message reminder fix.
2. **Permissions, activity and durable foundations:** IMP-34–37, 39–40, 43 and the urgent phone boundary IMP-45; then IMP-44. Migrate incrementally with rollback and isolation checks before relying on new automation.
3. **Agency delivery:** IMP-07–08, 13–20. Build scope/allowances, service templates, batches and review experience before more production automation.
4. **AI and creative production:** IMP-23–27. Depend on permissions, audit history and cost accounting; keep independent final approval.
5. **Business operations and integrations:** IMP-21–22, 28–33, 38. Implement local workflows independently; use real connections only when configured and authorized.
6. **Operational acceptance:** IMP-41 throughout, with final backup/restore, outage, capacity and production rollout acceptance after prior batches.

This order is adjustable when dependencies or evidence require it. Individual items may span multiple batches.

## Progress and execution log

### 2026-10-25 — autonomous implementation checkpoint

- **IMP-05 complete locally:** My tasks now groups revisions, blockers, overdue work, current work, upcoming work, undated work, reviews and approved work. Priority and dates determine ordering without changing assignments. Reviews do not remain in an actionable overdue list. Approved work stays reachable and explicitly distinct from client fulfilment. Compact personal task cards show scheduled dates separately from deadlines, blocker notes, next actions and real checklist progress. Filters start collapsed when unused.
- **Design acceptance:** inspected 1440px and 390px layouts. The first browser pass exposed oversized cards and hidden scheduled dates; both were corrected before acceptance. Verified approved filtering opens its group and a mobile task opens the correct drawer. Eight focused behavior tests pass, including date/priority ordering, filters, immutable input, required action, checklist progress and task opening. Existing navigation tests are being updated for the concurrent draft-protection implementation.
- **IMP-43 verified foundation:** typed GraphQL feed and append-only transactional events; two generated additive migrations verified up/down and applied locally. Task/agent names snapshot at the event, alongside departed-member/project/client identity. Person/date/client/project/action/outcome filters, cursor pagination, before/after details, source navigation and explicit coverage limits are implemented. Desktop/mobile browser checks passed, including person + outcome filtering, empty results, clearing and source links.
- **Audit source tests:** rollback-only suites pass for workspace isolation, revoked/inactive access, identity matching, strict filters/cursors, date boundaries, same-time pagination with intervening insert, actor/subject filtering, no read writes, secret exclusion and atomic failure. Business/client suites pass structural records, receipt/void/reconfirmation, unsent drafts, portal links/shares/forms/assets and budget requests. Client-triggered task creation now attributes the client portal rather than falsely crediting its authorizing manager. Creative and live Meta sources remain under implementation, so IMP-43 remains Partial.
- **Build health:** fixed the existing Meta lead-intake update type mismatch and rebuilt stale shared/SDK outputs. Direct frontend and backend typechecks pass on this checkpoint. Existing hook/Meta regression tests (64) passed. No new paid calls, external messages, ad changes, production deployment or GitHub push. All database acceptance suites roll back; temporary browser fixtures are explicitly tracked for cleanup.
- **Next work already active:** protect unsaved task drafts; complete creative/Meta activity sources; then continue durable operations, scoped permissions and remaining agency workflows in dependency order. No additional phase approval is required.

### 2026-10-25 — IMP-05 daily work decision

- **Problem/outcome:** staff need to know which assignment to act on next without interpreting a general board. Amna's planning should translate into a clear personal queue, with revisions and blockers visible and approved work still retrievable.
- **Workflow:** assigned work → attention/today/upcoming/unscheduled/review/approved groups → plain next-action cue → existing task detail and submission/review workflow. Sorting and grouping must never change assignments, scheduled dates or approval state.
- **Alternatives/design:** use the existing My tasks destination with compact grouped lists, rather than introduce another dashboard or automatic rescheduling. Put required action and due date ahead of decorative metrics. Separate waiting-for-review and approved work from actionable production; make all groups reachable through existing filters and links. Keep the board available where useful.
- **Cross-system/acceptance:** reuse current project permissions and independent final approval. Test dates, priorities, revisions, blockers, waiting reviews and completed work, including deterministic ordering and empty/filtered results. Inspect the actual employee journey on desktop/mobile and ensure managers' planner/review views are unaffected.

### 2026-10-25 — IMP-43 advertising event decision

- Founder and Amna need to distinguish an automatic import, a person requesting a refresh, a connection change and a human report decision. Record only committed lifecycle events in the same transaction as the reporting state, preserving report identity and the marketer affected by review. Repeated identical imports do not invent new reports or repeat review events.
- Use the existing scoped feed and internal advertising links; expose schedule, mapping, date and counts, never tokens, raw API payloads or recommendation text. A stable workspace/account-derived UUID identifies legacy connections without changing credentials or migrating the JSON contract. Manual import authorization is rechecked inside its claim transaction; automated imports are labeled System rather than credited to the founder.
- Verify mocked provider failures, revoked/stale access, duplicates, in-flight disconnect, manager review/assignment and rollback; no live ad account changes or network calls are needed for acceptance. Scheduler catch-up and normalized report storage remain IMP-38/34 work.

### 2026-10-25 — IMP-43 activity implementation decision

- **Problem/outcome:** the founder cannot reliably answer who changed work, what Amna approved or what an agent actually did without opening several screens. Build a durable, searchable timeline that reduces investigation and handoff time; do not infer effort or productivity from clicks.
- **Workflow:** task/project changes, independent review, fulfilment, role changes and agent execution → structural event saved in the same transaction → administrator filters person/client/project/date/outcome → opens changes and source work. Failed transactions must not leave success events. Human initiators, automated work and approvers remain distinct.
- **Alternatives:** aggregating mutable JSON histories would be quicker but loses identity, ordering and retention guarantees. A new append-only event table with bounded, indexed reads preserves names at the time of the event and creates a reliable basis for later AI analysis. Existing histories remain where they are; no invented backfill or claim of complete historical coverage.
- **Design:** add one Team activity destination under Business, with compact filters and chronological entries. Put actor, action, time, outcome and work context ahead of detail; expand structural before/after changes on demand. Provide explicit loading, empty, retry, revoked-access and pagination states on desktop/mobile. Avoid leaderboard, time-worked or activity-score displays.
- **Permissions/tradeoffs:** initially restrict the company-wide feed to active administrators with fresh server authorization and session identity checks. Preserve workspace isolation and snapshot departed staff names. Store selected structural fields, not inbox bodies, prompts, credentials, client assets or raw provider errors. A short shared access lock prevents a role-revocation race during reads; larger storage/locking refactors remain separately tracked.
- **Acceptance before completion:** transaction rollback, workspace/non-admin/revocation isolation, same-time pagination, date/person/client filtering, identity preservation, read-only behavior and absence of sensitive payloads; real browser navigation/filter/details/empty/error checks at desktop and narrow widths. Remaining domain sources will be stated explicitly, and IMP-43 stays Partial until its full acceptance scope is met.

### 2026-10-25 — additional role requirements

- IMP-39 now explicitly includes multiple simultaneous roles and combined effective-access previews.
- Phone access is a separate capability: Sales, Setter, Admin and Secondary Admin receive the default grant; Marketing alone and all other roles do not. Enforce UI and calling APIs, with revocation and audit evidence. This is queued for the permissions foundation; a hidden dialer is not completion.

### Permission foundation investigation

- Native Twenty currently allows one authorization role per person/workspace (role-target unique index and singular role cache/evaluation). Multiple returned role entries must not be presented as working native multi-role access.
- Proposed bounded approach: retain the native data-security role; introduce explicit OS business-role IDs, many assignments per person and capability grants with provenance. Effective capabilities combine assigned roles while existing object/client scope and independent approval restrictions remain. Never infer permission from editable job labels.
- Phone enforcement must cover token minting, dialer/device lifecycle, current outbound-call authorization, incoming recipient filtering and recording access. Existing tokens require server-side rechecks after role revocation; a hidden button alone is insufficient.
- No permission assignment, schema migration or phone restriction has been implemented in this first usability slice. Next security slice must include membership, union/renaming, self-escalation, revocation and cross-workspace tests.

### 2026-10-25 — IMP-39/45 decision before implementation

- **Problem/outcome:** staff may combine jobs, but an editable title currently grants nothing and every signed-in member can initialize the phone. The founder needs understandable delegation without exposing client records or allowing unrelated staff to call. Success means a combined-role preview matches actual server decisions, and removing phone access blocks new calls even with an old device token.
- **Current → proposed workflow:** a single access dropdown plus job label becomes named business roles, multiple assignments, and a preview of each effective capability and its source. Administrators edit one person or role at a time, review the resulting access, and save explicitly. Existing project membership still controls ordinary staff work; final approval remains independent of the assignee.
- **Alternatives/chosen approach:** native Twenty role targets and caches currently enforce one CRM security role per person. Changing that entire authorization engine in the urgent phone fix risks widening record access. Use normalized OS roles/assignments as an additional capability layer and retain native CRM object/record permissions. Do not infer authority from labels or pretend that multiple native CRM roles already work. Start with the three boundaries actually enforced today: OS administration, delivery management and phone use. More granular client/inbox/agent permissions remain IMP-39 work.
- **Design:** People and Roles views, compact summaries, one focused editor, checkboxes for multiple roles and a readable access preview with provenance. Explain inherited CRM administrator rights separately. Keep errors next to the action, retain drafts on conflict, offer reload/cancel, show loading and denied access, and verify keyboard and narrow layouts. Do not add a giant permission matrix for capabilities that the backend does not implement.
- **System effects/tradeoffs:** fresh active membership checks; role changes use revisions and a serialized transaction with audit before/after; reject self-assignment changes and administrator lockout. Legacy ADMIN/MANAGER grants map to stable default role IDs until explicitly replaced, never to editable names. Default Sales/Setter/Admin/Secondary Admin have phone access; other roles do not. Read-only defaults avoid database writes during permission reads. Calling checks token creation, signed outbound webhooks, incoming recipients and recordings, retaining native record scope. Device cleanup handles logout/workspace changes/revocation. Credential settings remain separately protected.
- **Acceptance planned:** database rollback tests for union, renaming, explicit revocation, stale edits, inactive/foreign membership, self-escalation, administrator preservation and audit evidence; calling tests for old-token denial and native scope; frontend behavior tests for previews, failures and device cleanup; actual Team screens at desktop/narrow widths, with no paid calls. Generate reversible migration and inspect its scope before applying locally. No production deployment implied.

### Retrospective of dependent IMP-01/12 under the new review rule

- **IMP-01:** inconsistent parallel menus made discovery harder for staff. One grouped desktop/mobile navigation model was chosen over adding another dashboard; it preserves native CRM routes and explains the active page. The recorded desktop/390px and keyboard walkthroughs support this narrow navigation acceptance. Remaining role-specific defaults and full accessibility audit are explicitly IMP-02/11, not claimed complete by IMP-01. New roles must preserve this navigation structure.
- **IMP-12:** opening a task silently marked unrelated messages read and could suppress the founder's requested reminder. Scoped assignment visibility plus explicit per-message read/ack actions preserves accountability without requiring extra acknowledgement for work genuinely opened. Rollback tests cover hidden messages, revocation and the once-only five-minute overnight boundary; the corresponding UI tests confirm loading/restricted views do not mark work read. This fixes reminder correctness, not the separate durable dispatch requirement in IMP-35.


### 2026-10-25 — IMP-39/45 implementation and acceptance

**Implemented behavior and design:**

- **IMP-39 (partial):** Team & projects now has People/ Roles views, multiple business-role assignments and editable role capabilities. The current enforceable boundaries are OS administration, delivery management and phone use. The combined preview explains each grant's source, including protected native CRM administrator access. Role names never grant authority. Admin, Secondary Admin, Sales and Setter grant phone use by default; other default roles do not. Legacy explicit ADMIN/MANAGER access maps to stable role IDs until a person's roles are explicitly replaced.
- Permissions are normalized in four additive core tables. Member revisions and selected-role revisions prevent stale overwrites and outdated previews; role/assignment changes write before/after audit events in the same transaction. The shared delivery lock serializes changes with delivery writes. Self-assignment changes and loss of one's own administrator authority are rejected; existing independent final approval remains enforced even for an Admin-role assignee.
- Delivery actors, reviewer eligibility and staff selectors now use effective capabilities and active membership. Disabled/deleted users, removed memberships and inactive workspaces are denied. A pending member without a native CRM role receives no inherited administrator grant without breaking everyone else's directory; unrelated permission/database errors still propagate.
- **IMP-45 (locally complete):** phone controls and initialization require phone.use. The server checks token creation, current signed outbound caller access (including diagnostics), incoming recipients and recording access. Old voice tokens cannot authorize a new request after revocation. Native contact/record/application permissions remain in the ORM context for contact association and recording reads; hidden explicit contacts are rejected. Twilio credential settings remain separately guarded. Signed callbacks for already-created calls still update their records.
- Role management, interactive delivery GraphQL and voice-token minting require session access tokens; delegated applications cannot inherit unrestricted user capabilities through these endpoints. Scoped application/agent delegation needs explicit future implementation, not an assumption that a user ID is sufficient.
- Frontend permission refresh uses one loader for the whole app every 15 seconds, rather than one poller per phone field. It fails closed on identity mismatch/errors and cleans up devices on logout, workspace change or revocation, including late registration/token races. Server checks remain authoritative between refreshes. Already active provider calls or previously emitted ringing instructions are not remotely terminated by role changes; local device cleanup occurs on the next access update.
- **UI/UX refinements:** compact adjacent People/Roles and Save/Cancel controls; truthful inherited-admin summaries; keyboard-operable multi-selects; grant provenance; explicit save/cancel; draft preservation and selected-role revision checks; translated duplicate/conflict/policy errors next to Save; reload retains edits and never submits them. Page refresh now includes role/access queries. Native project forms remain available.
- **IMP-40/43 (partial foundations):** role operations have typed GraphQL inputs/results and generated frontend contracts. Permission changes have transactional audit records with actor, subject, before/after and time. This does not yet provide the requested all-work person-level activity viewer or AI analysis.

**Verification and evidence:**

- 52 focused backend calling/guard tests passed, covering token denial, old-token authorization rechecks, incoming filtering, native contact/recording scope handoff, delegated-application rejection and permitted session access. Provider responses are mocked; no billable calls or recording fetches were made.
- 22 focused frontend tests across seven suites passed, including role unions/provenance, conflict recovery, denied/stale identity, retained drafts, device teardown/races and a 20-consumer check proving one permission query. Twelve workspace tests passed, including the expanded Refresh action and existing navigation/task behavior.
- All eight rollback PostgreSQL regressions passed: access, delivery, business, Meta, operations, clients, agents/Redis and creative. Added tests cover disabled/inactive/foreign membership, native-role onboarding gaps, unexpected-error propagation, defaults, overlapping grants, renaming, stale previews, denied legacy commands, audit evidence, rollback after rejected self-revocation and independent approval.
- A separate two-connection test passed: concurrent assignments and role updates each produce exactly one commit, one 409 and one audit row. Delivery was observed waiting behind an uncommitted revocation, then returned 403 without persisting a project. Its temporarily committed isolated workspace/schema/users were removed and cleanup verified; existing application/default-role rows were untouched.
- Generated current-version migration `1792916611500` was narrowed to the four new tables/indexes/foreign keys; unrelated schema drift was removed before applying. Up/down passed in a rollback transaction. Only this new migration was pending; it was applied through the repository CLI locally. The CLI's standard --force option was needed for an existing older workspace-upgrade marker, not to skip migration failures. Server build and metadata generation passed.
- Changed-source lint/format and diff checks passed. Direct frontend/backend typechecks contain only the previously recorded four front-component AppPath errors and two Meta lead-intake DeepPartial errors; the whole repository is not claimed typecheck-clean.
- Actual browser checks at 1440px and 390px verified role catalog, protected current-admin access, keyboard capability changes, implicit delivery management from OS administration, Editor + Sales phone provenance, Editor + Marketing denial, duplicate-name feedback and preserved drafts. Saving/reopening Editor + Marketing for a temporary unprivileged test member succeeded and its audit event was verified. The exact member/user/assignments/test audit event were removed afterward; the default role catalog remains initialized. Real staff permissions were not changed. Temporary viewport override was reset.

**Limits and next work:**

- IMP-39 remains partial: only the three existing OS boundaries are dynamic. Per-client/project/conversation/finance/agent capability design and consistent enforcement across the older REST/file/search/job surfaces still need work. Twenty's native CRM security role is still singular.
- Native scope handoff was verified in local automated tests; configured real-role RLS/provider behavior, live inbound/outbound/recording acceptance and staff acceptance remain production verification under IMP-41. No live call, external message, paid AI request, ad change, GitHub push or production deployment was performed.
- IMP-43 needs the actual person/date/client activity interface and remaining work-event sources. IMP-44 needs scoped, cited AI analysis after that foundation and budget controls. Continue these with the mandatory workflow/design review, alongside the unfinished usability items. No original phase 5–10 is marked complete by this checkpoint.

### 2026-10-25 — IMP-10 inbox and creative draft decision

- **Problem and workflow:** an unsent client reply can currently disappear when a conversation revision changes, and a brief or assembly plan can disappear when a creative document refreshes or the editor switches stages. Protect typed work from entry through save, conflict recovery, stage changes and departure; do not turn unsaved text into a client send or a creative approval.
- **Alternatives/design:** use controlled, in-memory drafts with a saved baseline and the opened server revision, reusing the workspace navigation guard. This is preferable to revision-key remounts, a blanket dirty-on-change flag, or browser-storage autosave: reverting an edit restores a clean state, unrelated refreshes preserve dirty inputs, and client content/prompts are not persisted to local/session storage. Clean forms may adopt fresh data. Dirty conflicting forms keep their text and require an explicit reload before saving.
- **Scope and relationships:** begin with Inbox replies and creative brief/assembly forms, then apply the same pattern to client/business editors. Guard local selection, Cancel, stage replacement and navigation; preserve failed writes. Distinguish a successful mutation followed by a failed refresh from a failed mutation, to prevent accidental duplicate work. Continue enforcing server revisions and existing scope/review permissions.
- **Acceptance:** behavior checks for refresh while dirty, changing values back to the baseline, same-page replacement, Keep/Discard, failure/retry, current-revision reload and successful-save cleanup; desktop/mobile browser checks before completion. File uploads and forced browser/crash recovery are separate concerns and are not claimed as protected by persisting browser drafts. The tracker status remains Partial pending the broader form audit.

### 2026-10-25 — IMP-10 remaining editor decision

- **Business/workflow review:** finish the same protection across project/access setup, creative supporting forms, business decisions/follow-up assignment and assistant configuration/run inputs. These forms affect permissions, client decisions and paid-work preparation; a preview or selection must not be mistaken for a committed save or completed action.
- **Chosen design:** reuse the single workspace guard and compare editable values with their saved baseline. Guard local editor/stage/assistant replacement as well as route departure; hold navigation during a write, preserve failed inputs and retain opened versions for existing versioned records. Keep ordinary viewing filters clean. Confirming an assistant input preview does not mark its inputs saved; a successfully queued run may do so. Starter-template changes replace configuration and therefore need the same discard choice.
- **Tradeoffs/acceptance:** use explicit reload for stale editing snapshots, maintain existing independent-review and capability checks, and test Cancel/Keep/Discard, revert-to-clean, failed writes, stale versions and paid-run preparation without actually running models. Projects currently lack a server revision field; frontend snapshot-change detection cannot replace a future server concurrency contract. Creative uploads and generated work keep their existing server/job lifecycle; no resumable upload or crash recovery is claimed. Parent performs real-browser verification before recording completion.

### 2026-10-25 — IMP-35 durable reminder decision and backend acceptance

- **Problem and business outcome:** a slow Telegram request held the shared delivery transaction, delaying unrelated task updates and approvals. Staff still need the requested single five-minute reminder, at all hours, while the OS stays responsive and accurately distinguishes delivery from uncertainty.
- **Current → proposed workflow:** the scheduler stores a unique reminder outbox row with the notice in a short transaction, then queues its ID after commit. The existing Redis worker claims it in another short transaction, rechecks current membership/project scope, assignment, read/acknowledged state and bot configuration, commits `SENDING`, performs HTTP with no transaction open, and records the result in a final short transaction. Recovery requeues unsent durable rows and marks abandoned sending claims uncertain.
- **Alternatives and design:** reusing the existing queue and a small normalized outbox avoids both the network-under-lock bottleneck and a broad rewrite of all Operations data. A queue-only record could be lost between committing the notice and enqueueing; a unique database record survives that gap. Automatically retrying an ambiguous Telegram result could duplicate the founder's once-only ping, so only pre-send queued work is safely recoverable. The UI retains the existing notice context with a Queued state and explicit uncertainty requiring manual checking; there is no new acknowledgement ceremony.
- **Cross-system tradeoffs:** private payloads, bot tokens and chat IDs remain out of queue/outbox storage. A scope/read change before claim suppresses the send; a change after the final preflight or during HTTP cannot unsend an already dispatched message. Notifications contain a generic authenticated link. Legacy pending notices enter the queue normally; legacy sending notices are conservatively made uncertain by the existing scheduler. Terminal-row retention and the remaining Operations history cap belong to IMP-34.
- **Functional acceptance:** `check-delivery-operations.cjs` passed existing read/ack, once-only overnight reminders, revoked access, recurrence, fulfilment and activity regressions with mocked Telegram. `check-delivery-reminder-outbox.cjs` passed using separate real PostgreSQL connections plus an isolated Redis worker: HTTP observed no active worker transaction; a real task edit committed while HTTP was held; duplicate workers and recovery produced no duplicate sends; enqueue outage, read/ack/revocation, missing configuration, rejection, timeout, crashed claim and lost receipt were handled as specified. Persisted payloads excluded secrets. The committed sentinel workspace/schema/users and temporary queue were removed and cleanup verified. No live Telegram sends occurred.
- **Schema and checks:** generated additive command `1792924760229` passed local up/down and unique-notice checks and was applied by the normal migration workflow. Server build, direct typecheck and focused source lint passed. Actual configured bot/recipient and production worker acceptance remain separate; no live employee messages or production deployment are claimed.

### 2026-10-25 — IMP-34 read-only snapshot decision

- **Problem and workflow:** business, client and advertising snapshots called the same transaction helper as writes, persisting the full Operations JSON and initializing missing domains during page views. Delivery and follow-through reads avoided saves but resolved access and records through separate unlocked managers. A refresh should produce a current authorized view without creating records, changing timestamps or interfering with another person's work.
- **Chosen incremental approach:** introduce a shared advisory-lock read transaction on the existing workspace key. Readers can coexist; an exclusive task/role/Operations edit serializes with authorization and the data read. Reuse the current transaction manager for internal reads during mutations, preventing a second-connection lock deadlock. Read existing state or construct ephemeral defaults, with no repository save. Keep actual command writes, Meta import leases/heartbeats, link responses and uploads as writes. Separate portal/asset reads and Meta connection preflight reads from saving transactions too.
- **Alternatives/design rationale:** removing locks entirely would make a permission check and its response race project/role revocation. Normalizing every domain in one change would create unnecessary migration and integration risk. Shared locked reads preserve the existing screens, defaults, empty states and permission rules while eliminating refresh-induced writes and allowing independent readers. Access default roles are deterministic in-memory values; they are materialized only by explicit role/assignment changes, not snapshots. Native CRM permission caching is unchanged.
- **Acceptance and limits:** scoped source lint and direct server typecheck passed. A new isolated two-connection regression is prepared to verify absent rows/defaults remain absent; existing JSON, `xmin`, timestamps and activity remain identical; forbidden/failed reads do not write; credentials/assets preserve scope; concurrent reads coexist; and real edits/project/role revocations are observed after commit. Database execution evidence will be appended after the latest combined build. This slice does not normalize the remaining JSON domains, remove exclusive write serialization, provide retention, or complete all of IMP-34.

### 2026-10-25 — IMP-34 snapshot acceptance evidence

- `check-delivery-snapshots.cjs` passed against the latest combined server build. It calls Delivery, Follow-through, Business, Clients and Meta snapshots repeatedly for authorized and restricted staff, plus valid/invalid portal credentials, public/staff brand assets and a shared creative asset. It compares exact Operations/native/domain JSON, PostgreSQL `xmin`, timestamps and activity before/after, and captures executed SQL to reject data writes. An absent Operations row stays absent; an existing legacy row keeps missing domain keys absent; reading virtual default roles creates no role rows. Invalid identity, disabled membership, forbidden reads, unavailable assets and a mocked failed Meta connection preflight produce no writes.
- Actual separate PostgreSQL connections proved that two readers coexist, a real task edit waits behind a reader then commits, and reads waiting behind actual project/role revocation observe the committed removal/denial. There are no dirty reads or response-driven saves. The fixture temporarily commits only a uniquely marked workspace/schema/users, verifies its sentinel before deleting, and confirms exact cleanup afterward. No real staff/client data or external accounts were changed.
- Existing delivery workspace, Operations, Clients and Meta rollback workflow scripts all passed. After review found a secondary Telegram repository lookup inside Operations snapshot, that read was moved onto the same transaction manager; the snapshot/concurrency and Operations regressions passed again. The new read helper rejects an inactive supplied transaction manager, and recurring-template validation explicitly reuses its existing write transaction.
- Focused six-file type-aware lint, formatting, server compilation and direct server typecheck passed; the final Operations lookup passed its focused lint and regression rerun. No GraphQL contract or UI change is required: the existing pages retain their permitted fields and defaults, now without persistence caused by viewing them. Native CRM permissions still use the existing permissions/cache path. Normalization, history retention, independent write locking and the remaining IMP-34 domains remain open.

### 2026-10-25 — IMP-06/09 client engagement setup decision

- **Workflow and issue:** ordinary video/SEO engagements currently expose advertising budget setup that does not apply to them, and known missing project/name/service requirements are left for submit-time validation. Performance marketing requires a project manager and a different marketer who belongs to the selected project.
- **Chosen design:** keep the engagement basics visible and reveal advertising settings only when budget requests are enabled. Retain those values in the same in-memory draft when toggled off/on; omit them from the save payload while disabled. Show inline prerequisites and disable Save until the visible requirements are valid, including client-visible scope and onboarding question labels. Offer only project members for marketer selection and preserve existing server validation and independent manager review.
- **Alternatives and acceptance:** a separate advertising wizard would fragment this short setup, while unmounting uncontrolled fields would erase choices. Controlled validation values plus a hidden, disabled advertising fieldset provide progressive disclosure without draft loss. Test ordinary engagement setup, missing project/services, optional advertising, same-person rejection, membership filtering, off/on retention and save payloads. Do not create staff or save a real engagement during verification.

### IMP-26 — production AI financial guard: decision and verification scope (2026-10-25)

- **Business outcome and workflow:** run counts do not protect Otogrow's margins when models, prompt sizes, retries and failures have different costs. The chosen flow is administrator-verified prices and explicit opening spend → task/inbox/media reservation → current permission and source-version check under the transaction lock → committed dispatch claim → provider call outside the lock → usage reconciliation independent of whether output is accepted, discarded or fails validation. Human final approval stays separate. No real cap, balance, tariff or new spending authorization is seeded; the earlier USD 2 instruction is not treated as fresh funding or an inferred remaining balance.
- **Alternatives and decision:** post-call accounting alone cannot stop concurrent overspend; a run-count cap is retained only as an operational limit. The new core ledger uses exact decimal micro-USD, workspace and optional client/task caps, immutable original reservations/settlements and append-only financial evidence. Missing prices or disabled caps block paid dispatch. A conservative full-model-context ceiling avoids invented character/token conversions; administrator-supplied bounds and tariffs are identified as administrator-verified, not automatically verified. Actual provider overruns are fully recorded. Late provider evidence retains the original settlement and increases counted exposure when it is higher. Unknown outcomes keep their reservation until evidence-based reconciliation.
- **UI/UX and design rationale:** company money settings belong with administrator AI controls; employees need the maximum hold, readiness and a clear setup/blocking reason at the run action. Maximum reserved cost and actual charge are distinct. Empty setup must not suggest money is available; a saved policy is not the same as an enabled, priced workflow. Reconciliation is offered only for uncertain charges and requires evidence/reason; cancelling an active call does not claim a refund. The visible name is **OS production AI spending**, because native CRM chat/search/agent execution is outside this ledger. The root implementation owns desktop/narrow-screen inspection and complete form/recovery evidence.
- **Cross-system effects and limits:** reservations and business writes share the existing delivery advisory lock so role/configuration changes and parallel run requests are serialized; provider I/O remains outside transactions. Durable holds survive process loss and worker retries. SEO public-page checks and local FFmpeg assembly need no paid AI reservation. Token-priced text and still-image inspection are the supported configuration surface. Image/video generation fails closed until its provider billing units and exact reservation model are supported; native CRM AI, provider billing integration and verified narrow request bounds remain separate work. The budget read service now computes exact scope totals in PostgreSQL and pages spend rows with an exact-timestamp keyset, so changing pages cannot change the reported cap exposure.
- **Acceptance:** nine exact-money/bounds policy tests pass, including cache-split rounding, decimal-equivalent tariff handling and unknown usage. All four final rollback scripts passed against the combined server build: dedicated ledger, task-agent, inbox/business and creative/media workflows. These cover authorization, caps, stale price previews, revocation at dispatch, failure/cancellation, immutable history, late evidence, filtered pagination and atomic rollback. The initial independent two-connection cap/race test passed; expanded aggregate and cursor precision verification is recorded by the coordinating task. Direct server typecheck passed and scoped lint reported zero warnings/errors. Generated four-table migration up/down and application passed through the normal instance CLI. No paid request, external message or real budget configuration was made. This is not a global-completion claim; final acceptance results are recorded by the coordinating task after the latest server build and browser checks.

### 2026-10-25 — IMP-10 and engagement form automated acceptance

- Implemented in-memory draft protection for Team project/role editing, assistant configuration/template changes and run inputs, business decisions/follow-up assignments and selected reply sources, creative references/shots/media reviews/variants/revision requests and upload preparation. Existing brief/assembly/inbox/client/business editor protection remains in place. Versioned actions use the revision opened for the draft; stale inputs remain visible and require explicit reload. A changed record cannot silently hide unsaved review notes merely because its next action is no longer available. Successful writes clear the relevant baseline; failures preserve input; pending writes hold navigation. Upload selection is protected before submission, without claiming resumable uploads or crash recovery.
- Native form snapshots include disabled named fields so an in-flight save cannot accidentally replace the baseline with empty FormData. Controlled extras track unnamed dynamic fields. Explicit media access configuration now uses the same protection. Removed the obsolete unknown-cost permission control; paid requests require pricing/budget checks, and image/clip generation is visibly unavailable until billing for those outputs is supported.
- Engagement advertising settings now appear only when enabled, retain choices when toggled, and are excluded from submission while disabled. Inline prerequisites and disabled Save cover client project, name, service count/length, scope, question labels and distinct eligible manager/assigned marketer. No real engagement or staff data was created for these checks.
- Automated acceptance: 49 tests across ten focused frontend suites passed, including real component Cancel/stage switching, failed-write retention, revision conflicts/reload, current-scope blocker cleanup, saving/navigation, source selections, project reset/revert, advertising disclosure/validation and blocked unsupported media generation. After refining unnamed-field snapshot handling, the affected three suites passed again (13 tests). Direct frontend typecheck, 19-file type-aware lint, the final two-file lint and diff checks passed. Browser acceptance is recorded separately by the parent task.
- Remaining IMP-10 work includes Operations settings/recurrence/message editors, Meta connection/report controls, client-share preparation and media run preparation. Projects still need an authoritative server concurrency revision. The route blocker audit found one hook, one provider mount and one delivery route; identity remount tests confirm cleanup without multiple-blocker warnings. No live provider call, client message, paid generation or deployment occurred.

### 2026-10-25 — IMP-26 independent review and concurrency acceptance

- Independent source review identified and corrected three concrete financial/authorization gaps before acceptance: source scope/configuration/cancellation is now rechecked in the same locked transaction as paid dispatch; reservation and actual token charges aggregate exact rational amounts before one final micro-USD rounding; and late provider evidence cannot disappear behind an earlier manual settlement. Higher late evidence remains counted against every applicable cap, while the original settlement and the additional evidence remain separate, durable records. Inspection also resolves an unavailable local model before dispatch so it does not falsely classify a request that never reached a provider as uncertain.
- `check-otogrow-ai-budget-concurrency.cjs` passed against the current combined build and applied migration using two independent real PostgreSQL sessions. Cross-source workspace, client and task races each yielded exactly one reservation within a shared cap. Distinct permitted requests coexist; duplicate source requests create one hold/event; concurrent dispatch creates one dispatch; source failure rolls back its hold and native change together. Stale cap/price edits produce one committed change and one conflict with one financial event.
- The same fixture observed real database lock waits for budget pause, project revocation and pre-dispatch cancellation, then verified dispatch was denied after commit without a dispatch event. Cancellation after dispatch retained its hold; missing usage remained uncertain; late usage settled a failed/discarded source; actual overshoot was fully counted and blocked subsequent spending. A later higher provider receipt after manual zero reconciliation increased counted exposure and was idempotent across duplicate evidence. Employee financial snapshots were denied and public snapshots excluded private source content and reconciliation evidence.

### 2026-10-25 — IMP-10 operational and connection drafts decision

- **Workflow:** protect schedule/settings edits, task messages and fulfilment notes, Meta connection/review drafts, approved-version sharing preparation and media request inputs. Ordinary expansion/read filters stay clean. Preparing a send/run is distinct from saving settings or actually sending; only a confirmed successful command clears the corresponding input.
- **Design and tradeoff:** reuse the workspace guard and native/controlled draft baselines. For authoritative versioned commands retain the opened revision. Settings, recurrence and some report decisions currently lack a server revision contract, so compare only their relevant source fields to detect observed refresh conflicts; exclude scheduler heartbeat/last-run counters. This provides explicit reload and preserves typed input, but cannot replace server concurrency checks for changes not yet observed. Sensitive token fields remain only in the live form and submission; draft tracking stores whether a password field is filled, not its value, and never uses browser storage.
- **Acceptance:** retain successful mutation versus failed refresh distinction to avoid accidental duplicate messages/shares; protect local replacement, Cancel and route departure; hold pending commands, preserve failed writes and avoid automatic retries. Verify with mocked commands and actual component tests. No external connection test, Telegram message, client share or paid model request will be sent during this work.

### 2026-10-25 — IMP-13 structured commitments decision

- **Company outcome:** Amna and the founder need a defensible view of what each engagement promised and what was actually fulfilled in its agreed period. Internal approvals, hook/CTA combinations and repeated deliveries must not inflate unique-video counts or hide remaining work.
- **Workflow:** manager creates an explicit retainer or one-time period with local dates and timezone, defines quantity lines, and links each task once as a unique video, a variation of a named unique video, or one other completed work item. Actual fulfilment confirmation earns one credit; client objection, production reopening or a newer unfulfilled submission removes the current credit while retaining history. Late work stays with its explicitly assigned original period. There is no automatic rollover or billing action.
- **Alternatives/design:** interpreting existing allowance prose would invent contractual quantities; counting approved tasks or generated assets would misstate client fulfilment. Keep legacy allowance notes unchanged, show them only to managers, and add normalized periods/lines/task allocations with append-only change history. The existing engagement page is the context, with promised/fulfilled/remaining displayed per line, unique videos and variations visibly separated, and an explanation for each uncredited task. Staff see structural work counts within existing project access, without commercial notes or prices. Portal summaries expose only the client's own aggregate commitments.
- **Change safety/tradeoffs:** optimistic revisions and required reasons protect edits, closed periods freeze plan edits, and a task can belong to only one commitment line at a time. Explicit task classification remains a human responsibility; the system cannot prove a video's originality from its file. Existing fulfilled work can be linked explicitly only when current approval/delivery/fulfilment evidence reconciles; uncertain history requires reconfirmation. Preserve records rather than deleting or guessing historical counts.
- **Acceptance required:** cross-workspace/project/identity/revocation checks; concurrent assignments and stale revisions; independent approval versus fulfilment; no duplicate credits; variation/parent rules; period boundaries and late completion; objection/reopen reversals; legacy notes preserved; atomic failed writes and durable history; desktop/mobile manager and staff journeys. This entry records the decision, not completion.

Parent checks responsive/browser workflows; no real commitment or billing mutations are part of acceptance.

### 2026-10-25 — IMP-14 service workflow decision

- **Business outcome:** Amna should define a service's repeatable quality requirements once, and editors, designers, SEO specialists, marketers and developers should see exactly what to finish and submit. The observable check is that required evidence cannot be omitted and a reviewer can identify the actual requirements used for each submitted version; reduced planning/rework time remains a staff-acceptance outcome to measure.
- **Current → proposed workflow:** current tasks contain free-text service/brief plus a generic checklist and links. A manager will choose or adapt an unsaved AI-video/static-ad/SEO/performance/web/app starter, publish an immutable template version, and explicitly apply it to a task. The task receives a pinned snapshot of required work, evidence types and definition of done. Staff record progress and evidence; the system validates completion and field/link format; the assigned independent manager checks substantive quality and required review checks. Final approval remains separate from client fulfilment.
- **Alternatives/design:** do not introduce a second scheduler or a rigid workflow engine with arbitrary executable rules. Reuse existing tasks, reviewer permissions, submissions, revisions and recurring task creation. Distinguish a reusable service workflow from a recurring schedule: recurrence may pin a published workflow version, and later edits affect neither existing tasks nor that explicit pin. Keep service requirements together in the task drawer with plain missing-item messages and show the pinned version. A manager catalog offers starters, editing, publishing and archiving; staff see only version context on permitted project tasks. Existing briefs, music/editor notes, custom checklists and evidence remain untouched.
- **Change safety:** every published definition is immutable. Applying a different version is explicit, requires current task/template revisions and a reason, preserves the prior applied snapshot, and resets its requirement responses instead of silently validating changed criteria. Submitted/approved tasks reject requirement edits unless the manager explicitly reopens production; reopening also removes current commitment credit. Archived templates remain readable on pinned work but cannot be newly applied or scheduled until restored. Legacy tasks keep existing behavior until a workflow is explicitly applied.
- **Verification boundary:** a completed checkbox or a syntactically valid link is not proof of factual accuracy, brand compliance, ranking gains or campaign performance. UI/API will explicitly label automatic checks as completeness/format checks and human review as the quality decision. Existing SEO agents may provide evidence, but this slice does not grant autonomous approval, spend, publishing or fulfilment authority.
- **Acceptance:** immutable versions and submissions, stale preview rejection, fresh manager/staff/project/workspace scope and revocation, required checklist/evidence/review checks, preserved task notes and legacy behavior, explicit reopen/credit reversal, archived-template behavior, pinned recurrence with no duplicate scheduling, atomic failures, and desktop/mobile manager/staff/reviewer journeys. This is an implementation decision, not a completion claim.


### 2026-10-25 — IMP-38 missed-day recovery and attribution refresh decision

- **Business/workflow:** a report for yesterday alone leaves holes after downtime and never notices delayed conversions on prior dates. Add explicit account-local coverage and a bounded recovery/refresh policy while keeping human campaign decisions and the actual account scheduler off pending its real staff mapping. No revenue, qualified leads or attribution is inferred beyond returned provider fields.
- **Design:** retain the existing short transaction/lease and provider fetch outside the lock, choosing one date per account per tick. Default automatic recovery covers 14 past days; seven recent days are eligible for a daily attribution refresh; all manual and scheduled attempts share a default ten-date daily limit. Administrators can configure recovery 1–90 days, refresh 0–30 days no larger than recovery, and a bounded daily attempt limit. Prioritize yesterday, then oldest missing dates, then least-recently-checked refresh dates. Empty successful responses count as checked, failures do not, and attempts are counted durably before network work. Hourly backoff, auth pause, fresh scope/mapping checks and stale-lease result rejection remain. Manual single-date recovery is explicit, strictly before the account-local current date, and cannot bypass the same daily cap/backoff.
- **Freshness/history:** retain bounded per-date successful-check metadata on each connection. Older connections derive known dates from compatible saved reports without read-time writes. Show the automatic window, missing dates, refresh still due, attempts and last successful check. A matching result updates check metadata without duplicating a report or notice; changed data creates a new review snapshot and preserves earlier approved metrics, recommendations, assigned tasks and history. Approved-only staff visibility remains, without exposing unreviewed values.
- **Tradeoffs/acceptance:** this is additive bounded JSON configuration, not a new provider integration or a replacement for report retention. The existing 500-report/10 MB guard must fail visibly rather than delete or overwrite approved history; longer-term archival under IMP-36 remains separate. Test Qatar/local-date boundaries, first-run and outage catch-up, rolling delayed data, repeat/overlapping ticks, empty responses, date ordering, daily limits including failed/manual attempts, backoff/auth failure, immutable approval and current access. Use only mocked provider responses; do not enable the actual connection or call Meta.

### 2026-10-25 — IMP-14 workflow interface decision

- Managers need a short route from a reusable service standard to a clearly assigned task, while staff need the exact requirements and evidence for their current work. A separate Work → Service workflows catalog keeps template maintenance out of the daily task queue and the already broad Team screen. Compared with an all-in-one task wizard, this keeps task creation simple and makes reuse/version history explicit. Six starters remain unsaved suggestions until a manager publishes one.
- Use one editor at a time, compact name/service fields and progressive checklist/evidence/reviewer sections. Publishing, archive/restore and applying a version require a reason and captured revisions. A changed or reopened workflow explicitly resets its responses while retaining prior applied snapshots; live tasks never silently adopt a catalog edit. Required evidence remains internal and separate from client-facing deliverable links.
- The task panel shows the pinned definition of done, producer checks/evidence and missing requirements. Submission remains blocked until saved requirements are complete; the assigned independent reviewer confirms required checks from the submitted snapshot. Completeness and link-format checks do not claim substantive quality verification or client fulfilment. Scoped staff cannot read the manager catalog.
- Reuse memory-only draft guards, identity-checked no-cache reads, safe permission-error states and authoritative mutation responses. Distinguish a successful save with failed wider refresh from a failed write. Acceptance covers immutable version choice, stale previews, failed writes/reload, scoped reads, saved/unsaved requirements, independent review, guarded navigation and desktop/mobile readability. This decision does not claim completion.

### 2026-10-25 — IMP-14 backend acceptance checkpoint

- Added typed manager workflow catalog/publish/archive APIs and scoped task application/progress/history APIs. Required quality-review checks must be explicitly confirmed by the existing independent reviewer. Completing workflow fields cannot submit, approve or fulfil work automatically. Link checks validate format/completeness and do not claim source accuracy or fetch URLs.
- Submitted/approved work is locked against progress/evidence edits. Explicit manager reopening, including reopening the same pinned version, archives the prior response snapshot, resets workflow responses, requests production changes and removes current commitment credit. Archived templates remain usable as pinned task context but block new applications and new scheduled occurrences until restored. Recurrence pins an immutable version and uses the existing scheduler/savepoint/idempotency logic; template publication does not rewrite that pin.
- Direct server typecheck, scoped type-aware lint, build and eight policy tests passed. The new rollback PostgreSQL suite passed immutable publication/task/submission snapshots, manager/assignee/observer/project/session/disabled-user scope, stale revisions, missing/invalid evidence, self-approval denial, explicit quality checks, same/new-version reopen and commitment reversal, archive/restore, old-version recurrence and no duplicates/orphan tasks, bounded history, read-only reads and atomic failure. Existing delivery workspace, operations, clients, creative, task-agent and commitment rollback suites also passed against the integrated build.
- The coordinating task generated, verified and applied the additive three-table migration. No real workflow definitions, tasks or staff settings were persisted; no external sends, provider requests or paid calls were made. Manager/staff/reviewer browser journeys and independent review remain part of overall IMP-14 acceptance, so backend evidence alone does not close the item.

### 2026-10-25 — IMP-38 backend acceptance

- **Implemented:** one account-local date is claimed per scheduler pass under the existing delivery lock: yesterday first, then the oldest missing date within the configurable 1–90 day recovery window, then recent dates whose provider values have not been checked that local day. Defaults are 14 recovery days, 7 refresh days and 10 import attempts per account/day. Existing connections retain their enabled/disabled state; the real Otogrow schedule remains off pending project/manager/marketer mapping.
- **Bounds and recovery:** manual yesterday imports and explicit past-date recovery share the same durable daily allowance and one-hour failure backoff as scheduled work. Every claimed attempt counts, including failure. Editing, disconnecting or reconnecting a connection cannot reset the same day's allowance or an outstanding backoff. Calendar validation excludes today/future dates in the account's timezone. Successful empty days count as checked, preventing endless no-data retries. Coverage identifies exactly the configured dates, missing dates, pending refreshes, next candidate and remaining attempts; it does not claim all-time completeness.
- **Preserved workflow:** HTTP remains outside workspace transactions. Claims/results still validate current owner, initiating admin, project/staff mapping, revision and lease. Unchanged provider data updates only separate freshness metadata; provider row/action reordering does not duplicate reports. Changed conversion values create a new manager-review version and notice. Existing approved figures, recommendations, approvals and assigned task remain byte-for-byte unchanged. Marketers see only their approved snapshot and a scoped indication that a newer version exists, without its unapproved values or hidden ID.
- **Acceptance:** 13 focused schedule/adapter tests passed, including Qatar midnight, leap/year and DST boundaries, legacy coverage without default writes, empty successful days, independent daily budgets, window bounds, action/row ordering and delayed values. Expanded `check-delivery-meta.cjs` passed against the current compiled backend with mock providers in an always-rolled-back PostgreSQL transaction: missed-day ordering, overlapping lease rejection, manual recovery outside the window, failed-attempt counting, quota/backoff across reconnect, independent approval/assignment, in-flight revocation/disconnect, old approved snapshot immutability, no duplicate notices on unchanged refresh, invalid date rejection, secret exclusion and explicit archival-cap failure. The mock provider asserts no service transaction is active during its HTTP path. `check-delivery-snapshots.cjs` passed again, including unchanged JSON/xmin/updatedAt/activity, absent rows/defaults staying absent, parallel read/write locking and fresh revocation. Direct server typecheck and scoped type-aware lint pass; combined build passed. No live Meta request, ad change or external message was made.
- **Remaining limit:** report history still uses the existing 500-report/10 MB workspace JSON limits. At capacity, the attempted date remains visibly incomplete with an archival error; no approved history is deleted. IMP-36 archival and broader IMP-34 normalization remain prerequisites for sustained production retention. Browser/design acceptance of the new controls and freshness copy is being completed separately; production scheduling still needs real staff mapping and operational acceptance.

Client objections, revisions and changed project/parent relationships remove invalid credit without erasing history. Late fulfilment stays in its assigned period.

Only explicitly tracked disposable fixture data was used; no client link or external message was sent.

### 2026-10-25 — IMP-15 production batch decision

- **Business outcome and complete workflow:** Amna needs to turn weekly/monthly plans into a visible group of existing tasks, inspect concepts and ownership, and finish independent reviews without reconciling another spreadsheet. A batch will reference one project, optionally its client engagement and an explicit commitment period. Managers create the plan, add existing tasks with their current revisions, and close/reopen the plan with a reason. Editors work in the existing tasks; reviewers use the existing exact-submission review path. Closing a batch closes planning, not delivery: corrections, client objections and later fulfilment remain visible.
- **Alternatives and chosen approach:** Project tags cannot retain membership/version history or explain changes, while copying tasks into a second production pipeline would split briefs, evidence and approvals. Use three normalized tables for batch heads, unique task memberships and immutable plan events. Keep project immutable after creation. A task may explicitly appear in a weekly and monthly view, but only once within each batch; cross-batch totals must not be added together as company output. No new scheduler, automatic task creation, bulk approval endpoint or duplicate fulfilment ledger is introduced.
- **Counts and cross-system effects:** Live rows retain each task's current owner, reviewer, concept, task/submission versions and status. Full creative version history stays in the linked task. Unique-video/variation/other classification comes only from an explicit current commitment allocation; otherwise show “Unclassified”. Internal approval and actual fulfilment are separate measures. Exact-version fulfilment uses the existing commitment evidence rules; commitment credit additionally requires a current matching allocation. Optional period context never allocates work automatically. A task moved outside the project, deleted, reopened, revised or reallocated cannot silently retain stale approval/credit; unavailable rows keep their original planning name without exposing another project's live details.
- **Design rationale and tradeoffs:** The intended Work → Batches journey shows a compact plan summary, clear open/closed state and separate approval/fulfilment counts, then paginated task rows with owner, concept and the next task action. Staff get their permitted project context; managers get planning controls and change history. Required reasons and optimistic revisions protect accidental replacement, and failures preserve the user's draft. Optional dates are local dates with an explicit timezone and exclusive end boundary, consistent with commitments. Existing task details own the full brief, workflow evidence and independent review checks; the batch links there rather than duplicating those forms or implying an unsafe “approve all” shortcut. Bounded pages keep response sizes predictable; counts cover the whole batch rather than the visible page.
- **Acceptance plan and remaining limits:** Verify create/edit/close/reopen, duplicate protection, stale previews and real concurrent edits, revoked manager/project access, cross-workspace isolation, unchanged legacy task lifecycle, live unit/approval/fulfilment distinctions, removed/deleted/moved tasks, immutable history and read-only reads. No real batch records will be seeded. This backend decision does not establish UI acceptance; desktop/narrow-screen planning and per-task review journeys remain required before completion.

### 2026-10-25 — IMP-15 implementation refinements

- Batch date fields are an optional **inclusive** start/end pair, matching what Amna sees on a calendar; commitment storage keeps its existing exclusive boundary. Lists/history use cursors, task choices use bounded search, and summary counts cover the whole selected batch. Only current submission metadata is loaded for counting; briefs/assets/full version history stay in task details. This bound supports weekly/monthly agency planning without returning an unbounded workspace payload.
- Each batch opens the existing independent task review, preserving exact submission, evidence and reviewer checks. No mass-approval shortcut or inferred fulfilment is introduced. Individual tasks can be corrected after the batch plan is closed; their live counters update.

### 2026-10-25 — IMP-36 Meta retention decision

- **Business outcome/workflow:** the current 500-report/10 MB shared-record guard eventually stops daily reporting. Store each imported snapshot independently, keep approved figures/recommendations/follow-up links, and let managers retrieve older decisions by account, date and status. Archiving hides a reviewed item from the active list; it never deletes evidence or changes approval. Pending reviews must be decided before archiving.
- **Chosen approach:** one normalized report table with indexed workspace/account/date/order/scope metadata, immutable provider figures and separately controlled review/archive state. Reads support legacy reports without writing; the next authorized Meta mutation migrates that workspace's legacy reports transactionally, verifying exact payload equality before removing the JSON copies. Conflicts abort the whole transaction. Schema migration is additive and reversible; no existing slow migrations are run. This avoids a broad unverified historical backfill while retaining existing report and notification IDs.
- **Design:** bounded report lists with explicit active/archive/all, account, date and decision filters; selected report details load separately. Keep saved recommendations and task links visible, preserve dirty review drafts across filter changes, show readable empty/error/loading states and explain archive/restore. Pagination and counts must reflect current permission scope. No auto-deletion or arbitrary retention period is invented.
- **System effects/tradeoffs:** update notification authorization and reporting coverage to use normalized records; maintain manager review before marketer visibility, fresh project membership, independent review, import leases, quotas/backoff and unchanged-import idempotency. HTTP stays outside transactions. Shared workspace locking remains until the broader IMP-34 work; other Operations history limits remain explicitly unfinished.
- **Acceptance:** legacy exact-copy/rollback/conflict checks, more than 500 retained reports, bounded list and ad detail pagination, date/status/archive filters, old approvals and follow-up links preserved, archived unchanged imports not duplicated, delayed conversions producing new versions, notification/revocation isolation, read-only reads, failed writes/drafts, and desktop/narrow browser inspection. No live Meta imports, paid calls, real staff changes or external messages are needed.

- **Implemented outcome:** Meta snapshots now live in a separate indexed table. The former 500-report guard no longer stops imports, and report payloads no longer enlarge the shared Operations JSON record. Approved figures, recommendations and follow-up task IDs stay unchanged when late conversion values create a new report version. Archiving is reversible visibility state and does not delete data or satisfy a pending review. Existing notices authorize against either legacy or normalized report identities.
- **Migration/recovery:** generated additive migration `1792942106514` creates one report table, four indexes and one workspace foreign key. Unrelated generator drift was removed before application. Schema up/down, exact legacy copy and conflicting-copy preservation passed in a rollback transaction; down refuses to remove a nonempty history table. Legacy reports remain readable without writes; the next Meta transaction, including a scheduler heartbeat, copies and verifies every legacy report before clearing its JSON copy. Denied/failed operations roll the move back. This is an application compatibility transition, not an automatic destructive downgrade.

Account, inclusive date, decision and active/archive/all filters are available; list counts cover the full permitted result. No real report was approved, archived or imported. The exact disposable reports and their test archive events were removed afterward.

Eight scheduling tests passed. The expanded rollback Meta suite passed growth beyond 500, original approval/task preservation, archive conflict and audit-failure rollback, current scope/revocation, legacy read-only/exact-copy/denied-write rollback, unchanged archived-import idempotency, bounded filters/cursors/details and ad pagination. The existing Qatar 06:00 schedule is still off pending actual project/manager/separate marketer assignment. No external Meta request, ad change, Telegram send, paid model call, role change, GitHub push or production deployment occurred.

### 2026-10-26 — IMP-39 finance permissions decision before implementation

Introduce separately assignable `finance.read` and `finance.manage` capabilities so accounting staff can inspect/export or record/confirm/correct entries without gaining delivery, phone, inbox or role administration. This is workspace-wide finance access, explicitly including salary allocations; client-limited finance roles are outside this slice.
- **Compatibility/authority:** existing OS administrators retain finance access through explicit capability inheritance; `finance.manage` includes reading. Delivery managers and project members gain nothing automatically. Role unions retain grant provenance, current membership checks, revision conflicts, last-admin protection and transactional activity. No real person's roles will be changed.

Denied and loading states must not reveal cached data. Finance project selectors expose only project names/IDs needed for allocation, without granting delivery task or client communication access.
- **Acceptance:** exercise real services against rolled-back data for read-only/write/union/revocation/isolation and unchanged project/inbox boundaries; focused UI tests for controls, inheritance and stale responses; desktop and narrow browser acceptance. No external provider calls or spending. IMP-39 remains Partial until its remaining scopes and surfaces pass their own checks.
