### 2026-10-31 — IMP-28 advertising decisions → accountable work decision

**Business outcome/current gap:** the daily report should help Amna/founder decide what to do, give each specialist a clear owned task and later show the observed result. Imported Meta and manually recorded reports currently use separate screens and review paths, with one generic follow-up task and no shared action/outcome loop. More charts or an AI-generated paragraph would leave that handoff unresolved. The next implementation is a single report→reviewed recommendations→explicit tasks→independent evidence review→observed result workbench, using existing reports and delivery tasks. No new credentials are needed for local engineering.

**Chosen workflow and alternatives:** provide one typed, scoped, bounded source list with explicit Imported Meta/Manually recorded labels, exact dated evidence, freshness, currency and review state. Keep account connection/scheduling controls separate. Source adapters preserve existing report visibility and original project/client identity; changed current routing must be checked rather than silently moving historical evidence. Managers choose/confirm the primary outcome and provenance, edit several actionable recommendations with rationale/expected result/evidence, choose a human owner and independent reviewer plus due date, then explicitly assign. A compatible existing assistant may be attached to help that person; it is not an employee identity or permission to run/change ad accounts autonomously. Reuse current task submission/review/fulfilment and budget controls.

A single checklist in the legacy generic task would obscure different owners/deadlines; a second campaign platform would duplicate source and delivery ownership. Choose normalized bounded action/review/observation records with immutable events and exact source/goal/routing snapshots. Stable command identities and scoped revisions must prevent duplicate tasks after concurrent/unknown replies. The source review path must preserve legacy behavior for legacy callers, while the new explicit action workflow avoids creating a hidden generic task. Existing follow-ups remain visible and preserved; adopting one must be explicit and must not overwrite its progress or manufacture another copy. Legacy assignment endpoints must not bypass the new workflow or create accidental duplicates.

**Metrics/learning:** never sum overlapping provider action types, infer qualified leads from Meta lead events, substitute commerce revenue for platform attribution, or treat task approval as proof of campaign improvement. Show unmapped/unknown metrics plainly. Manual observations/qualification require labelled human-provided evidence. Preserve the exact chosen source metric/goal/target/currency/window in the reviewed decision. Later observations link an exact later report or explicitly recorded evidence, retain corrections/history, and distinguish improved/worse/unchanged/inconclusive from causation. Optional creative references may link existing exact assets/tasks, but creative attribution and broader learning stay IMP-27.

**UI/UX:** the default Advertising destination should show reports needing review and assigned actions, not connection setup. Exact report/detail and action views keep source/date/goal/evidence, current owner, review state and next action clear. Use compact bounded lists, URL-owned filters/selection and source-owned drafts, an explicit assignment review, current task evidence links and a separate observation form. Preserve 320/390px, keyboard/focus, outage/revocation/unknown-save states. Reuse genuine scoped selectors and forms rather than fabricate a workspace or expose private costs/other clients. Connection/manual-entry compatibility paths stay reachable and honestly labelled while migration is incremental.

**Cross-system acceptance:** current role/project/source visibility before count/list/detail/write; source/routing/goal revision checks; independent review; exact-money/provenance/unknown handling; immutable source evidence; no duplicate native tasks/events under retry/races; original legacy task/report compatibility; no read writes or hidden workspace hydration on the new default entry; action task→evidence→review→observation for both source kinds; revocation/stale/partial-failure rollback; generated additive reversible schema with history-safe downgrade and protected source/fixture cleanup; actual desktop/narrow/draft/recovery journeys. New work does not authorize live ad/budget changes, paid inference, provider sends, schedule activation or deployment. **Advertising packet boundary agreed:** one explicit packet contains at most20 ready actions and assigns atomically. Assigned packet definitions/goal/source evidence stay immutable. The same exact report can later receive a new explicit follow-up packet; at most one DRAFT packet per source is allowed, with a bounded selectable packet history. This avoids inventing a replacement report merely to plan new work. Follow-up packets do not copy old actions/tasks by default or rewrite the original report approval. Legacy task adoption is globally unique and preserves existing task fields/progress. Native task briefs receive only the manager-reviewed action text/evidence, not an automatic dump of private report/account snapshots. Historical Meta normalization may have lost missing-field provenance: absent/ambiguous metrics stay unknown; new adapters must preserve any additional presence evidence without rewriting historical approved reports or inferring qualified leads.

**Advertising decision refinement:** a healthy report can be explicitly reviewed with zero actions only when a no-change reason is recorded; this avoids manufacturing employee tasks to clear reports. The same immutable receipt/source checks apply. A currently source-authorized manager may plan additional work against an already-approved report without rewriting its original independent approval. Existing legacy work requires an explicit choice: adopt one exact current task without overwriting its fields/progress, or acknowledge its current identity/revision/state and record why separate additional work is needed. ### 2026-11-02 — IMP-44/37 current-work evidence decision

**Company outcome and workflow:** recorded activity answers what happened; it cannot establish today's owner, waiting review or blocker. Add an explicit, initially unchecked current-work option to the same administrator question workflow. Person/project/client select the current task scope; audit dates/category/action/outcome remain history-only. Require a person, project or client scope and preview independent capture time, full matching count, omitted/truncated evidence and source citations before any funded request. Current person means assignee or the effective reviewer for the latest submission, including the current backup assignment; stale backups and self/unavailable reviewers must not be presented as an actionable review queue. Include approved tasks because delivery confirmation can still be pending. Never infer hours, productivity, individual ratings or automatically assign work.

**Architecture alternatives and decision:** reusing historical events would invent current facts; generic native task CRUD deliberately excludes managed delivery tasks; bypassing native permissions would widen access. Use a narrow readonly managed-task adapter that reuses current native object/field permission validation and existing RLS resolution/rendering while omitting only generic CRUD's delivery-task isolation. Unsupported visibility must fail closed. Project/client scope, current membership and review authority remain mandatory. Bound current facts independently (200 tasks/96 KiB), exclude briefs, copy, messages, media and monetary fields, and block funded work if matching evidence is incomplete. A source fingerprint excluding capture time binds preview/start/dispatch/completion; changed current facts require a new preview, and known in-flight usage is settled before stale output is discarded. Captured answers remain explicitly historical; opening a task citation rechecks its current visibility and never silently rewrites a saved answer.

**UI/UX and maintainability:** separate dated history from current work, label the different person semantics and time scopes before preview, and show current owner, due date, state, blocker and eligible primary/backup reviewer using familiar language. Exact current-task evidence opens in the existing page without losing the question; show both saved capture and fresh-check timestamps. Retain the older history-only body, receipts, prompt and output schema byte-for-byte via version dispatch, with false/omitted opt-in preserving the original canonical request. Reuse existing authority helpers, spending controls and readonly page shell rather than introduce another dashboard or independent agent runtime.

**Acceptance pending:** source/permission/reviewer behavior and compatibility tests; actual isolated SDK/current-source mutation/race proofs with mocks; direct types/lint/build/codegen; real desktop/mobile preview/citation/question-retention journeys and exact fixture cleanup. IMP-44 remains Partial and IMP-37 remains In progress until acceptance. No paid model call, external message, task change, role/ad/accounting action, Git push or deployment is part of this batch. Counts remain22 Local complete,15 Partial,1 In progress,8 Queued (24 unfinished).

**Refinements from dependent review:** saved answers must remain shared among currently authorized administrators. Recheck every captured task under the current reader's native object/field/RLS permissions instead of comparing the creator's identity-bound permission fingerprint; filter withheld runs before history counts and receipts, and preserve the stored record. Recheck the reader's permission fingerprint before returning a result. Title/project/member-name evidence now allows800 UTF8 bytes, covering valid200-character task/project labels without forcing staff to shorten ordinary non-Latin titles; the96 KiB aggregate cap remains. Free-form blockers retain an explicit512-byte bound, so oversized blocker text requires concise source metadata rather than a misleading narrower-scope retry.

The native permission adapter needs flat metadata/permissions/RLS maps, but never the ORM entity graph. Loading the full graph wastes memory and prevents a standalone readonly cache proof when that graph exists only in another process. Add an explicit `skipEntityMetadatas` context option used only by this adapter: keep all existing native authority maps, validators and SQL rendering, skip only unused ORM graph construction, and preserve default/lite behavior for all existing callers. Test both branches; never fake a feature flag or substitute permission data in the actual-workspace browser check. This is a memory/latency refinement, not a new authorization path.
